{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\widehat{X}\right|\geq\frac{\widetilde{C}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\alpha_{n}+\frac{1}{n^{\kappa/2}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{m}}\right)\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma}).

Proof.

By definition of medians, for any
x>0
, there is

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\widehat{X}\geq x\right)={\mathbb{P}}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)\leq\frac{m+1}{2}\right\}\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)\leq\frac{m+1}{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}<x\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha_{n}}{2(1-\alpha_{n})}-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}<x\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}<x\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha_{n}}{2(1-\alpha_{n})}+C_{\kappa}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}|X_{1}|^{2+\kappa}}{\sigma^{2+\kappa}n^{\kappa/2}}-\Phi\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}x}{\sigma}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}<x\right)\leq-\sqrt{\frac{c\log n}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}}\right\},

where the last line uses Berry-Esseen Theorem (Lemma
1
), and
x
is given by

x=\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}\Phi^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha_{n}}{2(1-\alpha_{n})}+C_{\kappa}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}|X_{1}|^{2+\kappa}}{\sigma^{2+\kappa}n^{\kappa/2}}+\sqrt{\frac{c\log n}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}}\right).

Now apply Lemma
2
for the i.i.d. sequence
{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}<x\right)
with

\mathfrak{E}\left\{1,{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}<x\right)\right\}\leq e,

we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}<x\right)\leq-\sqrt{\frac{c\log n}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}}\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma}),

with
c=4e\gamma
. Moreover, we have the following elementary facts

\Phi^{-1}(x_{0})=\Phi^{-1}(x_{0})-\Phi^{-1}\left(1/2\right)\leq\left(x_{0}-1/2\right)\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)^{\prime}(x_{0})\leq\frac{x_{0}-1/2}{\psi\left\{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2)\right\}},

holds for any
1/2\leq x_{0}<1-\delta/2
. Also we know
1/2\leq\Phi(\sqrt{n}x/\sigma)<1-\delta/2
holds for
m,n
sufficiently large. Denote
C_{\delta}=1/\psi\{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2)\}
, then there is

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\widehat{X}\geq\frac{\sigma C_{\delta}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{2(1-\alpha_{n})}+C_{\kappa}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}|X_{1}|^{2+\kappa}}{\sigma^{2+\kappa}n^{\kappa/2}}+\sqrt{\frac{c\log n}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}}\right)\right\}\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\widehat{X}\geq x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}<x\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}<x\right)\leq-\sqrt{\frac{c\log n}{(1-\alpha_{n})(m+1)}}\right\}\leq n^{-\gamma}.

Now do the same thing for
{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{X}\leq-x)
, we finally get the desired result.
∎

A.2
Proofs of the results in Section
2.2

We firstly provide the proofs related to the univariate VRMOM estimator.

Proof of Theorem
1
, Theorem
2
, and Theorem
3
.

Denote
\kappa_{1}=\min\{1,\kappa\}
and
\kappa_{2}=\min\{2,\kappa\}
. To prove this result, let’s firstly give a convergence rate for sample variance
\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma
. From the moment bound
{\mathbb{E}}|X_{1}-\mu|^{2+\kappa}<\infty
,
by Marcinkiewicz-Zygmund theorem (Theorem 5.2.2 in
Chow and
Teicher 2012
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}(X_{i}-\mu)^{2}-\sigma^{2}=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{2}/(2+\kappa_{2})}}\right),\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}(X_{i}-\mu)=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{2}/(2+\kappa_{2})}}\right).

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma\right|=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widehat{\sigma}+\sigma}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left(X_{i}-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{0}\right)^{2}-\sigma^{2}\right|

(32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}(X_{i}-\mu)^{2}-\sigma^{2}-\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{0}-\mu\right)^{2}\right|=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{2}/(2+\kappa_{2})}}\right).

Next pick out one summand in (
6
). Denote

\displaystyle G_{j}(x):={\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\mu)}{\sigma}\leq x\right\},\quad I_{j}(x):={\mathbb{I}}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\mu)}{\sigma}\leq x\right\},

then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widehat{\mu}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}-\frac{k}{K+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}-G_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}+\underbrace{G_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}-\Phi(\Delta_{k})}_{T}.

For the term
T
,

\displaystyle T=

\displaystyle G_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}-\Phi\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}+\Phi\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}-\Phi(\Delta_{k})

(33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(n^{-\kappa_{1}/2})+\psi(\Delta_{k})\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\psi(\Delta_{k})\frac{\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi(\Delta_{k})\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\alpha_{n}^{2}+\frac{\log n}{m}+\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{2}/(2+\kappa_{2})}}\right),

where line 1 to line 2 uses Berry-Esseen Inequality (Lemma
1
) and Taylor expansion, line 2 to line 3 uses (
32
) and concentration inequalities for

\widehat{\mu}
(Lemma
4
).
By using Lemma
4
and (
32
) again we have that

\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\alpha_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{m}}+\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{2}/(2+\kappa_{2})}}\right).

Also note that Berry-Esseen Inequality guarantees that
\sqrt{n}(Y_{j}-\mu)/\sigma
satisfies

|G_{j}(x_{1})-G_{j}(x_{2})|\leq\psi(0)|x_{1}-x_{2}|+2C_{\kappa_{1}}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}|X_{1}-\mu|^{2+\kappa_{1}}}{\sigma^{2+\kappa_{1}}n^{\kappa_{1}/2}}.

So we can apply Lemma
3
with
\delta_{n}=O(\alpha_{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{m}}+\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{2}/(2+\kappa_{2})}})
, which yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\left|\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\left[I_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}-G_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}-I_{j}(\Delta_{k})+G_{j}(\Delta_{k})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sup_{|x-\Delta_{k}|\leq\delta_{n}}\left|\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\left\{I_{j}(x+\Delta_{k})-G_{j}(x+\Delta_{k})-I_{j}(\Delta_{k})+G_{j}(\Delta_{k})\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\delta_{n}\log n}{m}}+\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{1}/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{n}\log n}{m}}+\Big(\frac{\log n}{m}\Big)^{3/4}+\frac{\log^{1/2}n}{m^{1/2}n^{\kappa_{2}/(4+2\kappa_{2})}}+\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{1}/2}}\right).

Thus it implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left[I_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}-G_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}\right]

(34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left\{I_{j}(\Delta_{k})-G_{j}(\Delta_{k})\right\}

\displaystyle+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{n}\log n}{m}}+\Big(\frac{\log n}{m}\Big)^{3/4}+\frac{\log^{1/2}n}{m^{1/2}n^{\kappa_{2}/(4+2\kappa_{2})}}+\frac{1}{n^{\kappa_{1}/2}}\right).

Again from (
32
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma}{(m+1)\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left[{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widehat{\mu}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\frac{k}{K+1}\right]\right|

(35)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{K|\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma|}{\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{(3\kappa_{2}+2)/(2\kappa_{2}+4)}}\right).

Combining equations (
33
) (
34
) (
35
), we have

\displaystyle\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\mu$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,-\mu=

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}-\mu-\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{(m+1)\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widehat{\mu}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\frac{k}{K+1}\right\}

(36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}-\mu-\frac{\sigma}{(m+1)\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}\left(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widehat{\mu}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\frac{k}{K+1}\right\}

\displaystyle+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n^{(3\kappa_{2}+2)/(2\kappa_{2}+4)}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\left[G_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}-I_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}}{\sigma}\Delta_{k}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n^{(3\kappa_{2}+2)/(2\kappa_{2}+4)}}+\frac{\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\{G_{j}(\Delta_{k})-I_{j}(\Delta_{k})\}

\displaystyle+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n^{(3\kappa_{2}+2)/(2\kappa_{2}+4)}}+\frac{\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}\right).

From central limit theorem, we have

\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\mu$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,-\mu=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}}+\frac{1}{n^{(3\kappa_{2}+2)/(2\kappa_{2}+4)}}+\frac{\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}\right),

(37)

which proves Theorem
2
. Moreover, when
m=o(n^{2\kappa_{2}/(\kappa_{2}+2)})=o(\min\{n^{2\kappa/(\kappa+2)},n\}),\log^{3}n=o(m)
and
\alpha_{n}=o(1/\sqrt{m})
, the remainder in (
36
) will be of the order
o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}})
, while the major term becomes a summation of i.i.d. sequence with

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\{G_{j}(\Delta_{k})-I_{j}(\Delta_{k})\right\}\right]=0,\>\text{ and}

\displaystyle\text{Var}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\{G_{j}(\Delta_{k})-I_{j}(\Delta_{k})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{K}G_{j}\left\{\min(\Delta_{k_{1}},\Delta_{k_{2}})\right\}-G_{j}(\Delta_{k_{1}})G_{j}(\Delta_{k_{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{K}\min(\tau_{k_{1}},\tau_{k_{2}})\{1-\max(\tau_{k_{1}},\tau_{k_{2}})\}+O(n^{-1/2}),

where the last line again follows from Berry-Esseen theorem (
1
). Applying standard central limit theorem we have

\sqrt{N}(\,\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\mu$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,-\mu)\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{K}^{2}),

where
\sigma^{2}_{K}
, as defined in (
9
), tends to
\pi\sigma^{2}/3
according to Lemma
6
in Appendix
B
. Therefore Theorem
1
is proved.

To prove Theorem
3
, we just apply (
37
) to each coordinate and obtain that

|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{\mu}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,-\boldsymbol{\mu}|_{2}=\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{p}|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\mu$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{l}-\mu_{l}|^{2}}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{mn}}+\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n^{(3\kappa_{2}+2)/(2\kappa_{2}+4)}}+\frac{p^{1/2}\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}\right).

∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

To prove asymptotic normality of multi-dimensional VRMOM estimator, using (
36
) and the rate constraints, for each coordinate
l
(where
1\leq l\leq p
), there is

\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\mu$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{l}-\mu_{l}=\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{l,l}}}{\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\{G_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})-I_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})\}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{pmn}}\right),

where

\displaystyle G_{j,l}(x):={\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j,l}-\mu_{l})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{l,l}}}\leq x\right\},\quad I_{j,l}(x):={\mathbb{I}}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j,l}-\mu_{l})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{l,l}}}\leq x\right\}.

For any vector
|\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}=1
, there is

\displaystyle\left\langle\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{\mu}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,-\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sqrt{\sigma_{l,l}}\big\{I_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})-G_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})\big\}v_{l}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}}\right).

Now we apply central limit theorem and yield

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{(m+1)n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{\boldsymbol{v}}}\big\langle\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{\mu}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,-\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{v}\big\rangle\xrightarrow{d}{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

\displaystyle\text{where }\quad\widetilde{\sigma}_{\boldsymbol{v}}^{2}=\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm T}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}}\boldsymbol{v}.

Here
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times p}
has its
(l_{1},l_{2})
-entry defined as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{l_{1},l_{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}}{\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})\}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\{I_{0,l_{1}}(\Delta_{k})-G_{0,l_{1}}(\Delta_{k})\}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\{I_{0,l_{2}}(\Delta_{k})-G_{0,l_{2}}(\Delta_{k})\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}}{\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})\}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\left\{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j,l_{1}}-\mu_{l_{1}})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}}}\leq\Delta_{k_{1}},\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j,l_{2}}-\mu_{l_{2}})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}}\leq\Delta_{k_{2}}\right)-G_{0,l_{1}}(\Delta_{k_{1}})G_{0,l_{2}}(\Delta_{k_{2}})\right\},

Moreover, we can apply multivariate Berry-Esseen theorem (See Theorem 1.3 in
Götze 1991
) and give

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{l_{1},l_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{l_{1},l_{2}}+O(n^{-1/2}),

where
\mathcal{C}_{l_{1},l_{2}}
is defined in (
14
). Thus the theorem is proved.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1
.

For the asymptotic normality of multi-dimensional MOM estimator, together with Lemma
5
below and the rate constraint, we can show that

\widehat{\mu}_{\mathrm{MOM},l}-\mu_{l}=\frac{\sqrt{2\pi\sigma_{l,l}}}{(m+1)\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\left\{G_{j,l}(0)-I_{j,l}(0)\right\}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{pmn}}\right).

For any vector
|\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}=1
, there is

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}-\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle=\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}}{(m+1)\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sqrt{\sigma_{l,l}}\big\{I_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})-G_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})\big\}v_{l}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}}\right).

Now we apply central limit theorem and yield

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{(m+1)n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{\boldsymbol{v}}}\big\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}-\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{v}\big\rangle\xrightarrow{d}{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

\displaystyle\text{where }\quad\widetilde{\sigma}_{\boldsymbol{v}}^{2}=\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm T}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}\boldsymbol{v}.

Here
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times p}
has its
(l_{1},l_{2})
-entry defined as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{MOM},l_{1},l_{2}}=

\displaystyle 2\pi\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}\;{\mathbb{E}}\left[\{I_{0,l_{1}}(0)-G_{0,l_{1}}(0)\}\{I_{0,l_{2}}(0)-G_{0,l_{2}}(0)\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}\left\{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j,l_{1}}-\mu_{l_{1}})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}}}\leq 0,\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j,l_{2}}-\mu_{l_{2}})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}}\leq 0\right)-G_{0,l_{1}}(0)G_{0,l_{2}}(0)\right\},

Moreover, we can apply multivariate Berry-Esseen theorem (See Theorem 1.3 in
Götze 1991
) and give

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{MOM},l_{1},l_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{MOM},l_{1},l_{2}}+O(n^{-1/2}),

where
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{MOM},l_{1},l_{2}}
is defined in (
17
). Thus the proposition is proved.
∎

Lemma 5
.

Let
N=(m+1)n
i.i.d. random variables
X_{1},...,X_{N}
evenly distributed in
(m+1)
subsets
{\mathcal{H}}_{0},\dots,{\mathcal{H}}_{m}
. There is a subset of index
{\mathcal{B}}\subset\{1,\dots,m\}
with
{\mathrm{C}ard}({\mathcal{B}})=\lfloor\alpha_{n}m\rfloor
. Define

\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{j}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}X_{i}\quad&j\notin{\mathcal{B}},\\
*\quad&j\in{\mathcal{B}},\end{cases}\quad\widehat{\mu}=\mathrm{med}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{j}\mid 0\leq j\leq m).

Suppose
X_{1}
satisfies
{\mathbb{E}}(X_{1})=\mu,\mathrm{Var}(X_{1})=\sigma^{2}
, and
{\mathbb{E}}|X_{1}-\mu|^{3}<\infty
. The fraction
\alpha_{n}
satisfies
\alpha_{n}\leq 1/2-\delta
for some
\delta>0
. Then
\widehat{\mu}
admits the following representation:

\widehat{\mu}=\mu-\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}{(m+1)\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\mu)-{\mathbb{P}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\mu)\right\}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log^{3/4}n}{m^{3/4}n^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{n}\right).

Proof.

Using Taylor expansion we have

\displaystyle\Phi\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}\right\}-\Phi(0)=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)+O(n(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\alpha_{n}^{2}+\frac{\log n}{m}\right),

(38)

since
\widehat{\mu}-\mu=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{mn}})
by Lemma
4
. On the other hand, denote

G(x)={\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}-\mu)}{\sigma}\leq x\right\},\quad I_{j}(x)={\mathbb{I}}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\mu)}{\sigma}\leq x\right\}.

From Berry-Essen theorem (Lemma
1
) we know Lemma
3
is applicable and yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}I_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}\right\}-G\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}\right\}-\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}I_{j}(0)+G(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\alpha_{n}+\frac{\log^{3/4}n}{m^{3/4}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

By definition of median we know

\Phi(0)=\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}I_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}\right\}+O\left(\frac{1}{m}\right).

Thus

\displaystyle\Phi\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}\right\}-\Phi(0)=

\displaystyle G\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}\right\}-\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}I_{j}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}-\mu)}{\sigma}\right\}+O\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\{I_{j}(0)-G(0)\}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\alpha_{n}+\frac{\log^{3/4}n}{m^{3/4}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(39)

Combining (
38
) and (
39
) we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}=

\displaystyle\mu-\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}{(m+1)\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\left\{I_{j}(0)-G(0)\right\}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log^{3/4}n}{m^{3/4}n^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu-\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}{(m+1)\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\mu)-{\mathbb{P}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\mu)\right\}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\log^{3/4}n}{m^{3/4}n^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{n}\right),

which is exactly what we want to prove.
∎

Appendix B
Positive Definiteness of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{MOM}}-\mathcal{C}

From Theorem
4
and Proposition
1
, in order to show that our proposed multi-dimensional VRMOM estimator

\boldsymbol{\mu}

has higher statistical efficiency than the corresponding MOM estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
, it is equivalent to prove that
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}\preceq\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}
holds true. In this appendix, we will verify that the covariance difference
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}
is positive definite in dimension 2 as
K
tends to infinity. First of all, we shall give a general formulation for the entry
\mathcal{C}_{l_{1},l_{2}}
as
K\rightarrow\infty
.

Lemma 6
.

Denote
\mathcal{C}_{l_{1},l_{2}}^{K}
as the
(l_{1},l_{2})
-entry of the matrix
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}
defined in (
14
). Then we have

\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{C}_{l_{1},l_{2}}^{K}=\Big\{4\pi\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(y_{1})\psi(y_{2})F_{l_{1},l_{2}}(y_{1},y_{2}){\mathrm{d}}y_{1}{\mathrm{d}}y_{2}-\pi\Big\}\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}.

(40)

In particular, when
l_{1}=l_{2}=l
, we have

\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{C}_{l,l}^{K}=\frac{\pi}{3}\sigma_{l,l}.

(41)

Proof.

For the denominator in (
14
), we compute that

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})=

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\frac{K+1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi\Big(\Phi^{-1}\Big(\frac{k}{K+1}\Big)\Big)\frac{1}{K+1}

(42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\psi\big(\Phi^{-1}(x)\big){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle(\text{change variable }x=\Phi(y))=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi^{2}(y){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-x^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}x=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi}}.

For the numerator, on the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{K}\tau_{k_{1}}\tau_{k_{2}}=

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k}\Big)^{2}

(43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{k}{K+1}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{0}^{1}x{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{2}=\frac{1}{4}.

On the other hand, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{K}\tau^{l_{1},l_{2}}_{k_{1},k_{2}}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}F_{l_{1},l_{2}}(\Phi^{-1}(x_{1}),\Phi^{-1}(x_{2})){\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}

(44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(y_{1})\psi(y_{2})F_{l_{1},l_{2}}(y_{1},y_{2}){\mathrm{d}}y_{1}{\mathrm{d}}y_{2},

where
F_{l_{1},l_{2}}(y_{1},y_{2})={\mathbb{P}}(Z_{l_{1}}\leq y_{1},Z_{l_{2}}\leq y_{2})
. Combining (
42
), (
43
) and (
44
) we have

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{C}_{l_{1},l_{2}}^{K}=\Big\{4\pi\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(y_{1})\psi(y_{2})F_{l_{1},l_{2}}(y_{1},y_{2}){\mathrm{d}}y_{1}{\mathrm{d}}y_{2}-\pi\Big\}\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}.

(45)

In particular, when
l_{1}=l_{2}=l
, we have

F_{l,l}(y_{1},y_{2})=\Phi(\min(y_{1},y_{2})).

Substitute it in (
44
), we can obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{K}\tau^{l,l}_{k_{1},k_{2}}=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(y_{1})\psi(y_{2})\Phi(\min(y_{1},y_{2})){\mathrm{d}}y_{1}{\mathrm{d}}y_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(y_{1})\int_{-\infty}^{y_{1}}\psi(y_{2})\Phi(y_{2}){\mathrm{d}}y_{2}{\mathrm{d}}y_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(y_{1})\Phi^{2}(y_{1}){\mathrm{d}}y_{1}=\frac{1}{3}.

Therefore we have

\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{C}_{l,l}=\frac{1/3-1/4}{1/(4\pi)}\sigma_{l,l}=\frac{\pi}{3}\sigma_{l,l},

which completes the proof.
∎

Verification of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}\preceq\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}
.

In the case of dimension
2
, we assume the gradient
\nabla f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=(\nabla_{1}f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),

\nabla_{2}f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))^{{\mathrm{T}}}
has covariance matrix

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{1,1}&\sin\phi\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}}\\
\sin\phi\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}}&\sigma_{2,2}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{therefore, }\quad\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1,2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\sin\phi\\
\sin\phi&1\end{pmatrix}.

From Lemma
6
, we have that
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{MOM},l,l}-\mathcal{C}_{l,l}=\pi\sigma_{l,l}/6
as
K\rightarrow\infty
, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1,2}=

\displaystyle 4\pi\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(y_{1})\psi(y_{2})F_{1,2}(y_{1},y_{2}){\mathrm{d}}y_{1}{\mathrm{d}}y_{2}-1/4\right\}\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\pi\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(y_{2}){\mathrm{d}}y_{2}\int_{-\infty}^{y_{2}}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{y_{1}}\psi(y_{1})\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2}){\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}y_{1}-1/4\right\}\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\pi\left\{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(1-\Phi(x_{1})){\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{y_{2}}\psi(y_{2})\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2}){\mathrm{d}}x_{2}{\mathrm{d}}y_{2}-1/4\right\}\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{4\pi\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Phi(-x_{1})\Phi(-x_{2})\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2}){\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}-\pi\right\}\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}},

where
\psi_{\phi}(\cdot,\cdot)
denotes the probability density function of the multivariate normal distribution with covariance matrix
\Sigma_{1,2}
, more precisely, we have

\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})=\frac{1}{2\pi\cos\phi}\exp\Big\{-\frac{x_{1}^{2}-2\sin\phi\cdot x_{1}x_{2}+x_{2}^{2}}{2\cos^{2}\phi}\Big\}.

By symmetry we clearly see that

\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})=\psi_{\phi}(-x_{1},-x_{2}),\quad\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},-x_{2})=\psi_{\phi}(-x_{1},x_{2})=\psi_{-\phi}(x_{1},x_{2}),\quad\Phi(x)+\Phi(-x)=1.

Then
\mathcal{C}_{1,2}
can be further simplified as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1,2}/\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\pi\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\big\{\Phi(-x_{1})\Phi(-x_{2})+\Phi(-x_{1})\Phi(x_{2})+\Phi(x_{1})\Phi(-x_{2})+\Phi(x_{1})\Phi(x_{2})\big\}\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2}){\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}

\displaystyle+4\pi\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\big\{\Phi(-x_{1})\Phi(x_{2})+\Phi(x_{1})\Phi(-x_{2})\big\}\big\{\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},-x_{2})-\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})\big\}{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}-\pi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\big\{1-2\Phi(x_{2})\big\}\big\{1-2\Phi(x_{1})\big\}\big\{\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})-\psi_{-\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})\big\}{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}.

Similarly,
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{MOM},1,2}
can be written in a similar form

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{MOM},1,2}/\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}}=\pi\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\big\{\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})-\psi_{-\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})\big\}{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}=\phi.

Therefore, to prove the positive definiteness of the matrix
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}
, it left to prove that

|\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{MOM},1,2}-\mathcal{C}_{1,2}|/\sqrt{\sigma_{1,1}\sigma_{2,2}}\leq|\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{MOM},1,1}-\mathcal{C}_{1,1}|/\sigma_{1,1}=\frac{\pi}{6}.

It is equivalent to the following inequality

\displaystyle|h(\phi)|:=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\phi}{\pi}-2\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\big\{1-2\Phi(x_{2})\big\}\big\{1-2\Phi(x_{1})\big\}\big\{\psi_{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})-\psi_{-\phi}(x_{1},x_{2})\big\}{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}\right|

(46)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6},

holds for all
\phi\in[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]
. In order to show this bound, we can draw the graph of
h(\phi)
numerically.

Figure 3:
The graph of function
h(\phi)
on the interval
[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
. We can see that
h
is increasing and has absolute value uniformly bounded by
1/6
.

As shown in Figure
3
, we can see that (
46
) holds true, which implies that
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}\preceq\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}
.
∎

Appendix C
Theories and Proofs for RCSL Estimator

This appendix consists of the theoretical results and proofs for the robust CSL estimator. In Appendix
C.1
, we present the technical assumptions and the main theories for the robust CSL estimator. The proofs of the results will be given in Appendix
C.2
.

C.1
Theoretical Results for Robust CSL Estimator

In this part, we present the theoretical results for the robust CSL estimator. Before that, we first introduce some notations and our technical assumptions.

C.1.1
Notations and technical assumptions

As we will demonstrate in Appendix
D
, all the technical assumptions hold on common statistical models, which suggests the wide applicability of these assumptions. For the loss function
f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta})
, we assume that
f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta})
is differentiable with respect to
\boldsymbol{\theta}
and denote
\nabla f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta}):=\nabla|_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta})
as the gradient of
f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta})
at
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. For a given
\boldsymbol{\theta}
, we define the expected gradient
\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
and population standard deviation of the gradient for each coordinate
l
,
\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
, as follows,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=(\mu_{1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),\dots\mu_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))^{{\mathrm{T}}}:={\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim{\mathfrak{X}}}\{\nabla f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta})\},

(47)

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}):={\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim{\mathfrak{X}}}\left[\{\nabla f_{l}(X,\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\}^{2}\right],\;\;\text{for}\;1\leq l\leq p.

For notational simplicity, we will denote the expectation taken over the randomness of
X
by
{\mathbb{E}}:={\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim{\mathfrak{X}}}
.
Recall that
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
is the true parameter that minimizes the loss function
{\mathbb{E}}f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta})
. Throughout the paper, we assume that
\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})={\mathbb{E}}\{\nabla f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}=\boldsymbol{0}
, which holds as long as the expectation and
\nabla
can be interchanged. Finally, for the ease of presentation, some parameter-independent constants such as
\rho,\eta,C_{M}
will be used across different assumptions when there is no confusion.

Assumption A
.

For every fixed
x\in{\mathfrak{X}}
,
f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta})
is a convex function of
\boldsymbol{\theta}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
.

Assumption B
.

There exists a constant
\rho>0
such that

\rho\leq\Lambda_{\min}\left[\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right]\leq\Lambda_{\max}\left[\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right]\leq\rho^{-1}.

Assumption C
.

The Hessian of the population loss
\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}
is Lipschitz continuous. In particular, there exists a constant
C_{H}>0
such that

\left\|\,\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2})\,\right\|\leq C_{H}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}|_{2},

holds for arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
.

Assumption D
.

For every
\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}
and
x\in{\mathfrak{X}}
, define

\displaystyle M_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}}(x,\boldsymbol{v}):=

\displaystyle\frac{|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\nabla f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-\nabla f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2})\rangle|}{|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}|_{2}},

\displaystyle\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$M$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,(x,\boldsymbol{v}):=

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}M_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}}(x,\boldsymbol{v}).

There exists
C_{M},\gamma_{0},\eta>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\{\eta M_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}}(X,\boldsymbol{v})\}\right]\leq C_{M},

(48a)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\exp\left\{p^{-\gamma_{0}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$M$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,(X,\boldsymbol{v})\right\}\right]\leq C_{M}.

(48b)

Assumption E
.

There exists a constant
\rho>0
such that

\rho\leq\min_{1\leq l\leq p}\{\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\{\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}\leq\rho^{-1},

where
\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
is defined in (
47
).

Assumption F
.

There exists
\eta>0
such that

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left\{\eta\left|\nabla f_{l}(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\right\}\right]\leq C_{M}.

(49)

Assumption G
.

The number of machines
m
, distributed sample size
n
, dimension of parameters
p
, and convergence rate
r_{n}
of the initial estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}
(i.e.
|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(r_{n})
) satisfy the following relationships

\displaystyle m=o(n),\quad p=O\left(\frac{n^{1/3}}{\sqrt{\log n}}\right),\quad r_{n}=O\left(\min\left\{\frac{1}{\log n},\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{p\log n}}\right\}\right).

(50)

Assumptions
A
and
B
assume the convexity of the loss and the local strong convexity of the population loss function around
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
, which are commonly assumed in empirical risk minimization literature. Assumptions
C
and
D
are the standard smoothness assumptions, which appear in distributed learning literature
(
Zhang
et al. 2013
;
Jordan
et al. 2019
)
.
In particular, the Lipschitz gradient assumption
D
is represented by two formulas (
48a
) and (
48b
) and the (
48b
) mainly handles the diverging dimensionality, which allows
p
to go to infinity. Similar conditions can be found in
Chen
et al. 2021
and
Su and Xu 2019
. An additional remark is that, our smoothness assumptions
C
and
D
are relatively weaker than those in existing literature. For example, the Assumption PD in
Jordan
et al. 2019
requires Lipschitz continuity of the second-order derivatives of the loss function
f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta})
. In contrast, we only require the expectation of the loss function
{\mathbb{E}}(f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}))
to be second-order differentiable and Lipschitz continuous (Assumption
C
), and allow the gradient
\nabla f
to be non-differentiable. Thus, our theoretical framework handles the Huber loss function as shown in Example
2
, while
Jordan
et al. 2019
can not.

Assumptions
E
and
F
guarantee concentrating properties for coordinate-wise gradient variance. In Assumption
F
we assume the gradients to be sub-exponential, which is weaker than the boundedness condition in
Alistarh
et al. 2018
and the sub-gaussian condition in
Yin
et al. 2019
. The sub-exponential condition is also assumed in
Chen
et al. 2017
and
Su and Xu 2019
. We note that the gradients can be sub-exponential in the case of least square regression (see Example
1
below for more details), which brings additional technical challenges in establishing concentration inequalities. In particular,
Yin
et al. 2018
imposed bounded absolute skewness (the third-order moment) condition, which is weaker than ours. However, their theory did not consider diverging dimension
p
.

The rate constraints on the quantities
m,n,p,r_{n}
are given in Assumption
G
. The relationships on
m
are inherited from Theorems
1
by letting
\kappa\geq 2
. The condition on
p
indicates that the dimension cannot diverge too fast. The final condition on
r_{n}
ensures that the initial estimator is consistent. It is worth noting that the constraint on the initial rate
r_{n}
is attainable. By definition, the initial estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}
is the minimizer of the local empirical loss function (
22
). From the regularity assumptions
A
–
F
, it is not hard to show that
|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(\sqrt{p\log n/n})
. Plug in the constraint on dimension
p
in Assumption
G
, we have that
|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(n^{-1/4})
. On the other hand, we can easily verify that
n^{-1/4}=O(\min\{1/\log n,1/\sqrt{p\log n}\})
. Therefore Assumption
G
can be satisfied.

We provide the following two examples for better understanding of the proposed assumptions. In Appendix
D
, we will verify that Assumptions
A
–
F
hold for generalized linear models and a large class of
M
-estimators.

Example 1
.

(Linear regression) In linear regression model
Y=\boldsymbol{X}^{{\mathrm{T}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\epsilon
, we assume that the covariate
\boldsymbol{X}=(X_{1},\dots,X_{p})^{{\mathrm{T}}}
and the noise
\epsilon
are both sub-gaussian random variables. Define
\boldsymbol{\xi}=(Y,\boldsymbol{X}^{{\mathrm{T}}})^{{\mathrm{T}}}
and the loss function
f(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\theta})=(Y-\boldsymbol{X}^{{\mathrm{T}}}\boldsymbol{\theta})^{2}
. Then we can compute the gradient of the loss function as

\nabla f(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=\epsilon\boldsymbol{X}.

In the
l
-th coordinate, the gradient
\nabla f_{l}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=\epsilon X_{l}
is a product of two sub-gaussian random variables, hence is sub-exponential (See Proposition 2.7.1 of
Vershynin 2018
).

Example 2
.

(Huber regression) In Huber regression model
Y=\boldsymbol{X}^{{\mathrm{T}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\epsilon
, similarly define
\boldsymbol{\xi}=(Y,\boldsymbol{X}^{{\mathrm{T}}})^{{\mathrm{T}}}
. The loss function is constructed as
f(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\theta})={\mathcal{L}}(Y-\boldsymbol{X}^{{\mathrm{T}}}\boldsymbol{\theta})
, where

{\mathcal{L}}(x)=\begin{cases}x^{2}/2\quad&\text{for }|x|\leq\delta,\\
\delta(|x|-\delta/2)\quad&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Then we can compute that

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(x)=\begin{cases}x\quad&\text{for }|x|\leq\delta,\\
\delta\,\mathrm{sign}(x)\quad&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}\qquad{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x)={\mathbb{I}}(|x|\leq\delta).

In this case, the Hessian
\nabla^{2}f(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\theta})=\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{X}^{{\mathrm{T}}}{\mathbb{I}}(|Y-\boldsymbol{X}^{{\mathrm{T}}}\boldsymbol{\theta}|\leq\delta)
is not continuous with respect to the parameter
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. However, if we assume the noise
\epsilon
has a symmetric distribution and uniformly bounded probability density function, we can prove that Huber regression model fulfills the proposed assumptions. The detailed verification is delegated to Appendix
D
.

C.1.2
Theoretical results

In this part, we provide the main theorems for the proposed RCSL estimator. We firstly provide single round convergence rate of RCSL estimator. To show the superiority in statistical efficiency of our VRMOM-based RCSL method, we present the asymptotic normality for our RCSL method and the MOM-based RCSL method. Then we will show that the VRMOM-RCSL method has smaller asymptotic variance than the MOM-based counter part.

Firstly, we present our estimation result for one round of communication in the following theorem, which helps understand the improvement from the initial estimator for only one iteration.

Theorem 6

(One-round convergence rate of the RCSL method)
.

Suppose Assumptions
A
-
G
hold and the initial estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}
satisfies
|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(r_{n})
. Further assume the fraction
\alpha_{n}
of Byzantine machines satisfies
\alpha_{n}\leq 1/2-\delta
for some fixed
\delta\in(0,1/2)
.
Then the robust CSL estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}
defined in (
19
) satisfies

|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{p^{1/2}\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right).

(51)

Applying Theorem
6
inductively, we can obtain the convergence result for our RCSL estimator with
t
rounds of aggregations, which is presented in Theorem
5
in Section
3
of the main paper.

Moreover, similar as Theorem
4
in the main paper, we can establish asymptotic normality for our RCSL estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)}
, which has not been studied in previous robust distributed learning literature. To save symbols and avoid repeated definitions, we denote
\sigma_{l_{1},l_{2}}={\mathrm{C}ov}\{\nabla f_{l_{1}}(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),\nabla f_{l_{2}}(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}
to be the
(l_{1},l_{2})
-entry of covariance matrix of
\nabla f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
, which is coincident with the notations in Theorem
4
. Then we can define
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}
exactly the same as in (
13
) and (
14
). Then we can prove the following asymptotic normality result:

Theorem 7

(Asymptotic normality of the RCSL method)
.

Suppose Assumptions
A
-
G
hold, and additionally, we assume the rate constraints
p=o(\min\{\frac{n^{1/3}}{\log^{2/3}n},\frac{m^{1/2}}{\log^{3/2}n},\frac{n}{m}\}),\alpha_{n}=o(1/\sqrt{mp})
, and
\log^{3}n=o(m)
. Then for every iteration number
t
satisfies (
24
) and any vector
\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
with
|\boldsymbol{v}|_{2}=1
, we have that

\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\sigma_{\boldsymbol{v}}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{v},\,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle\xrightarrow{d}{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

(52)

as
n\to\infty
, where

\sigma^{2}_{\boldsymbol{v}}=\boldsymbol{v}^{{\mathrm{T}}}\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}^{-1}\boldsymbol{v}.

(53)

Compared with the constraints in Theorem
4
, both of the theorems require the fraction
\alpha_{n}=o(1/\sqrt{mp})
, namely, the number of Byzantine machines is
o(\sqrt{m/p})
. However, the normality result of the RCSL method needs more restrictive constraints on the dimension
p
than the one in Assumption
G
and in Theorem
4
.

As we can see from (
53
), the asymptotic variance of the proposed RCSL estimator has a sandwich structure, which is commonly appeared in literatures (see,
e.g.
,
Polyak and
Juditsky 1992
;
Jordan
et al. 2019
;
Chen
et al. 2020
). However, since the past works only aggregate the gradients by sample mean, the centered covariance matrix in (
53
) is usually the covariance of the gradient, namely,
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}={\mathbb{E}}\{\nabla f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\nabla f(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})^{{\mathrm{T}}}\}
. In contrast, as we aggregate the gradient robustly by our proposed multivariate VRMOM estimator, the structure of the matrix
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}
is much more complex, as we can see in (
13
) and (
14
). To the best of our knowledge, this is the first asymptotic normality result in the setting of Byzantine-robust distributed learning.

In order to illustrate the efficiency of our RCSL method, it is possible to prove an asymptotic normality result for the median-of-means (MOM) based RCSL method, which is named MOM-RCSL. The explicit construction of the MOM-RCSL method is described in the paragraph before Section
4.2.1
.
We can show that the asymptotic variance of the MOM-RCSL estimator has the same formulation as (
53
), with
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{C}}}
replaced by
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathrm{MOM}}
in (
17
) of Proposition
1
. The proof technique is simply the combination of Proposition
1
and Theorem
7
. Therefore, we omit the presentation of this parallel result for brevity. The simulation results of comparison between our RCSL method and the MOM-RCSL method are already presented in Section
4.2
.

C.2
Proofs of the Results for Robust CSL Estimator

C.2.1
More Technical Lemmas

In the following, we introduce additional lemmas that will be used for proofing the results related to RCSL estimator. For consistency with Assumption
F
, we assume the random variables admits sub-exponential tail.

Lemma 7
.

(Quantile gap of median-of-mean) Let
N
(
=(m+1)n
) i.i.d. random variables
X_{1},...,X_{N}
evenly distributed in
m
subsets
{\mathcal{H}}_{0},...,{\mathcal{H}}_{m}
. Let
\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{j}=n^{-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}X_{i}
and
\widehat{X}^{\tau}
be the
\tau
-th quantile of
\{\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{0},\dots,\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{m}\}
. Suppose
{\mathbb{E}}(X_{1})=0,\mathrm{Var}(X_{1})=\sigma^{2}
, and
{\mathbb{E}}[e^{\eta|X_{1}|}]<\infty
for some
\eta>0
. There are two quantile levels
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}
satisfying
|\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}|=o(1)
and
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in(\delta,1-\delta)
for some
\delta\in(0,1/2)
. Then for every
\gamma>1
, there exists
\widetilde{C}
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\widehat{X}^{\tau_{2}}-\widehat{X}^{\tau_{1}}\geq\widetilde{C}\left(\frac{\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}\right)\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma}).

Proof.

Follow the proof of Lemma
4
, we can show that

{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\widehat{X}^{\tau_{1}}\right|\leq\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}\Phi^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\left|\tau_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\right|+\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}+C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{m}}\right)\right\}\geq 1-O(n^{-\gamma}),

(54)

holds for some
C_{1}
large enough. Denote

\delta_{n}:=\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}\Phi^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\left|\tau_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\right|+\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}+C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{m}}\right).

Evenly divide the interval
[-\delta_{n},\delta_{n}]
into
2n
pieces and define the set
{\mathfrak{N}}:=\{-\delta_{n},-\frac{n-1}{n}\delta_{n},\dots,\delta_{n}\}
. Similar as proof in Lemma
3
, we can find some
C_{2}>0
such that for every
\widetilde{x}\in{\mathfrak{N}}
, there is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sup_{\{x:|\widetilde{x}-x|\leq\delta_{n}/n\}}\left|{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x+y)-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x)-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widetilde{x}+y)+{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widetilde{x})\right|

(55)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left(|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\widetilde{x}-y|\leq\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\right)+\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left(|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\widetilde{x}|\leq\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\right)\leq C_{2}\left(\frac{\log n}{m}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

holds with probability
1-O(n^{-\gamma-1})
. For
0\leq j\leq m
, further define the random variable

Y_{j}(x,y):={\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x+y)-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x)-{\mathbb{P}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x+y)+{\mathbb{P}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x).

For every
x\in[-\delta_{n},\delta_{n}]
, there exist constants
C_{3},C_{4}
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x+y)-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x)\right\}\leq\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\frac{C_{2}\log n}{m}+\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}Y_{j}(x,y)\leq\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\frac{C_{2}\log n}{m}+\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-{\mathbb{P}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}\leq x+y)+{\mathbb{P}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}\leq x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}Y_{j}(x,y)\leq\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\frac{C_{2}\log n}{m}+\frac{C_{2}+C_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}-\Phi\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}(x+y)}{\sigma}\right)+\Phi\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}x}{\sigma}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}Y_{j}(x,y)\leq-C_{4}\left(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\frac{\log n}{m}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right],

by finding some
y>0
such that

\min_{x\in[-\delta_{n},\delta_{n}]}\left\{\Phi\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}(x+y)}{\sigma}\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}x}{\sigma}\right)\right\}\geq(C_{4}+1)(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})+\frac{C_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}+(C_{2}+C_{4})\left(\frac{\log n}{m}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(56)

Taking

y_{0}=\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\psi(\Phi^{-1}(\delta/2))}\left\{(C_{4}+1)(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})+\frac{C_{3}}{\sqrt{n}}+(C_{2}+C_{4})\left(\frac{\log n}{m}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right\},

since
\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}=o(1)
, and
m,n
tends to infinity, we can assume

\frac{\sqrt{n}(x+y_{0})}{\sigma},\frac{\sqrt{n}x}{\sigma}\in\left(\Phi^{-1}(\delta/2),\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2)\right),

always hold. Thus by applying mean value theorem to continuous function
\Phi(x)
, (
56
) can be guaranteed. Further compute that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-\delta_{n},\delta_{n}]}{\mathfrak{E}}\left\{1,Y_{j}(x,y_{0})\right\}=O\left(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\frac{\log n}{m}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

From Lemma
2
we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x+y_{0})-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x)\right\}\leq\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\frac{C_{2}\log n}{m}+\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]

(57)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}Y_{j}(x,y_{0})\leq-C_{4}\left(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\frac{\log n}{m}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]=O(n^{-\gamma-1}).

Combining (
54
),(
55
) and (
57
), finally we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\widehat{X}^{\tau_{2}}-\widehat{X}^{\tau_{1}}\geq y_{0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\sup_{x\in[-\delta_{n},\delta_{n}]}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x+y_{0})-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x)\right\}\leq(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})(m+1)\right]+O(n^{-\gamma})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n\max_{\widetilde{x}\in{\mathfrak{N}}}{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widetilde{x}+y_{0})-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widetilde{x})\right\}\leq\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}+\frac{C_{2}\log n}{m}+\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right]

\displaystyle+2n\max_{\widetilde{x}\in{\mathfrak{N}}}{\mathbb{P}}\Big[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sup_{\{x:|\widetilde{x}-x|\leq\delta_{n}/n\}}\left|{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x+y_{0})-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq x)-{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widetilde{x}+y_{0})+{\mathbb{I}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}\leq\widetilde{x})\right|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{2}\log n}{m}+\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big]+O(n^{-\gamma})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(n^{-\gamma}),

therefore prove the lemma.
∎

Lemma 8
.

(Stability of quantile with Byzantine machines) Let
X_{0},...,X_{m}
be fixed points, and
{\mathcal{B}}\subset\{1,\dots,m\}
be a subset of index with
{\mathrm{C}ard}({\mathcal{B}})=\lfloor\alpha m\rfloor
, where
0<\alpha<1/2
. Impose perturbation
\epsilon_{j}
on each
X_{j}
such that
|\epsilon_{j}|\leq\delta
for
j\notin{\mathcal{B}}
, and
\epsilon_{j}
can be arbitrary for
j\in{\mathcal{B}}
. Denote the
\tau
’th quantile of the three sets
\{X_{0},\dots,X_{m}\},\{X_{0}+\epsilon_{0},\dots,X_{m}+\epsilon_{m}\},\{X_{j}\mid j\notin{\mathcal{B}}\}
as
\widehat{X}^{\tau},\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}
and
\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},\tau}
respectively, Then there is

|\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}-\widehat{X}^{\tau}|\leq\left|\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},(\tau-\alpha)/(1-\alpha)}-\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},\tau/(1-\alpha)}\right|+2\delta.

Proof.

By definition of quantiles,
\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}
satisfies the following inequalities

\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left(X_{j}+\epsilon_{j}\leq\widehat{X}_{\epsilon}^{\tau}\right)\geq\tau(m+1),\quad\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left(X_{j}+\epsilon_{j}\geq\widehat{X}_{\epsilon}^{\tau}\right)\geq(1-\tau)(m+1).

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(X_{j}+\epsilon_{j}\leq\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}\right)\geq(\tau-\alpha)(m+1),\quad\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(X_{j}+\epsilon_{j}\geq\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}\right)\geq(1-\tau-\alpha)(m+1),

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(X_{j}\leq\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}+\delta\right)\geq(\tau-\alpha)(m+1),\quad\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left(X_{j}\geq\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}-\delta\right)\geq(1-\tau-\alpha)(m+1).

This implies that

\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},(\tau-\alpha)/(1-\alpha)}\leq\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}+\delta,\quad\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},\tau/(1-\alpha)}\geq\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}-\delta.

Similarly we can show

\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},(\tau-\alpha)/(1-\alpha)}\leq\widehat{X}^{\tau}\leq\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},\tau/(1-\alpha)}.

So we have
|\widehat{X}^{\tau}_{\epsilon}-\widehat{X}^{\tau}|\leq\left|\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},(\tau-\alpha)/(1-\alpha)}-\widehat{X}^{{\mathcal{B}},\tau/(1-\alpha)}\right|+2\delta
.
∎

Lemma 9
.

(Exponential concentration of variance) Let
X_{1},...,X_{n}
be i.i.d. random variables with
{\mathbb{E}}(X_{1})=0,\mathrm{Var}(X_{1})=\sigma^{2}
, and
{\mathbb{E}}[e^{\eta|X_{1}|}]\leq C
for some
\eta,C>0
. Construct sample mean
\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
and sample variance
\widehat{\sigma}^{2}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(X_{i}-\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,\right)^{2}
. Then for every
\gamma\geq 1
, there exists
\widetilde{C}
large enough, such that

{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma|\geq\widetilde{C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)=O(n^{-\gamma}).

Proof.

Define

\displaystyle\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}:=X_{i}^{2}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{X_{i}^{2}\leq C_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}\right\}-{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{i}^{2}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{X_{i}^{2}\leq C_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}_{i}:=X_{i}^{2}-\sigma^{2}-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}.

We can compute that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[X_{i}^{2}I\left\{X_{i}^{2}\geq C_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}\right\}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\int_{C_{1}\log n}^{\infty}2s{\mathbb{P}}(|X_{i}|\geq s){\mathrm{d}}s+C_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}{\mathbb{P}}(|X_{i}|>C_{1}\log n)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{C_{1}\log n}^{\infty}2Cse^{-\eta s}{\mathrm{d}}s+CC_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}n^{-\eta C_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{2\eta^{-1}CC_{1}\log n+2\eta^{-2}C+CC_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}\right\}n^{-\eta C_{1}}\leq n^{-\eta C_{1}+1},

for
n
sufficiently large. Then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{Y}_{i}\right|\geq 2n^{-\eta C_{1}+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|X_{i}|^{2}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{|X_{i}|^{2}>C_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}\right\}\right|\geq n^{-\eta C_{1}+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|X_{i}|^{2}\geq C_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n\max_{1\leq i\leq n}{\mathbb{P}}\left\{|X_{i}|\geq C_{1}\log n\right\}\leq Cn^{-\eta C_{1}+1}.

Take
C_{1}\geq\eta^{-1}(\gamma+1)
we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{Y}_{i}\geq 2n^{-\gamma}\right)=O(n^{-\gamma}).

(58)

Next we apply Bernstein’s inequality
Bennett 1962
for bounded random variables
\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}
. Together with the elementary inequality
{\mathbb{E}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}^{2})\leq{\mathbb{E}}(X_{1}^{4})\leq 5\eta^{-4}{\mathbb{E}}\{\exp(\eta|X_{1}|)\}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}\geq x\right)\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left\{-\frac{3nx^{2}}{6{\mathbb{E}}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}^{2})+2C_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}x}\right\}

(59)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left[-\min\left\{\frac{\eta^{4}nx^{2}}{20C},\frac{3nx}{4C_{1}^{2}(\log n)^{2}}\right\}\right]=O(n^{-\gamma}),

by taking
x\geq\eta^{-2}\sqrt{20\gamma C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
. Combining (
58
) and (
59
), we have proved

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{2}-\sigma^{2}\geq\eta^{-2}\sqrt{20\gamma C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}+2n^{-\gamma}\right)

(60)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}\geq\eta^{-2}\sqrt{20\gamma C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right\}+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{Y}_{i}\geq 2n^{-\gamma}\right)=O(n^{-\gamma}).

On the other hand, using the fact
x_{0}^{2}\leq e^{|x_{0}|}
we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\frac{\eta}{2},X_{1}\right)=\frac{4}{\eta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\frac{\eta^{2}}{4}X_{1}^{2}\exp\frac{\eta}{2}|X_{1}|\right\}\leq\frac{4}{\eta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left(e^{\eta|X_{1}|}\right)\leq\frac{4C}{\eta^{2}}.

Then Lemma
2
yields

{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,\right|\geq(\frac{4}{\eta^{2}}+1)\sqrt{\gamma C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma}).

(61)

Combining (
60
) and (
61
) we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\widehat{\sigma}-\sigma|\geq\widetilde{C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}-\sigma^{2}\right|\geq\sigma\widetilde{C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{2}-\sigma^{2}\right|+\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,^{2}\geq\sigma\widetilde{C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)=O(n^{-\gamma}),

by letting
\widetilde{C}
large enough.
∎

Lemma
9
directly implies that, under Assumption
E
,
F
and
G
, there is

{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\geq\widetilde{C}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma}),

(62)

holds every
\gamma>1
.

Lemma 10
.

(Uniform bound of variance difference) Under Assumption
D
,
E
,
F
and
G
, there exists
\widetilde{C}
large enough, such that

{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\geq\widetilde{C}r_{n}\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma}),

where
\Theta_{0}=\{\boldsymbol{\theta}:|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}\leq r_{n}\}
is defined in (
73
).

Proof.

From (
48b
) in Assumption
D
and Assumption
F
, we know

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})<p^{\gamma_{0}+1}\log n\;\right\}=1-O(n^{-\gamma}),

(63)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\max_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|<\eta^{-1}(\gamma+2)\log n\;\right]=1-O(n^{-\gamma}).

(64)

Construct the set of event

{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}:=\left\{X_{1},...,X_{n}\middle|\text{ (\ref{gradient_bound.ineq}), (\ref{sup_lipschitz_bound.ineq}) holds, and }\frac{\rho}{2}\leq\min_{1\leq l\leq p}\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}.

(65)

Together with (
62
), we know
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{X}}_{0})=1-O(n^{-\gamma})
.

Let
{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}
be the
n^{-M}
-net of
\Theta_{0}
, we know
{\mathrm{C}ard}({\mathfrak{N}}_{0})\leq(1+2n^{M})^{p}
. Then we will show

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}|_{2}\leq n^{-M}}|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})|\leq n^{-2}

(66)

always hold under the event
{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}
and
M
sufficiently large. Indeed, From (
64
) and (
63
), we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}|_{2}\leq n^{-M}}|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}|_{2}\leq n^{-M}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-g_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right\}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})-g_{l}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}|_{2}\leq n^{-M}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\Big\{\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\Big\}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-g_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})-g_{l}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\Big\}n^{-M}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2p^{\gamma_{0}+1}\log n}{n^{M}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}|_{2}\leq n^{-M}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\Big[2|\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|+2|g_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|

\displaystyle+\Big\{\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\Big\}\big(|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}+|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8p^{2\gamma_{0}+2}\log^{2}n}{n^{M}}\leq n^{-(M-2\gamma_{0}-4)},

Taking
M\geq 2\gamma_{0}+6
we obtain (
66
). Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|\geq 4x\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\geq\rho x,\;{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}|_{2}\leq n^{-M}}\left|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right|\geq\rho x,\;{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}+{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{X}}_{0}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p(1+2n^{M})^{p}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\geq\rho x,\;{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}+O(n^{-\gamma}).

The next lemma will show, when we take
x=O(r_{n})
, there is

\sup_{\{\boldsymbol{\theta}:|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}\leq r_{n}\}}{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\geq\rho x,\;{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}=O(n^{-p(M+\gamma+1)}),

and hence proves this lemma.
∎

Lemma 11
.

(Point-wise bound of variance difference) Under Assumption
D
,
E
,
F
and
G
, for every
\gamma>1
, there exists
\widetilde{C}
large enough, such that

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\geq\widetilde{C}r_{n},\;{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}=O(n^{-p\gamma}),

where
{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}
is the set of events defined in (
65
), and
\Theta_{0}=\{\boldsymbol{\theta}:|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}\leq r_{n}\}
is defined in (
73
).

Proof.

For every
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}
and
1\leq l\leq p
, there is

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right\}^{2}-\left\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}^{2}-\sigma^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\sigma^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big|}_{T}

\displaystyle+\left|\sigma^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\sigma^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|+\Big|\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Big\}^{2}

(67)

\displaystyle-\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big\}^{2}\Big|.

Let’s firstly deal with the term
|\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|
,

\displaystyle|\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|

(68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right\}^{2}-\left\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r_{n}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big\{2|\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|+r_{n}\left(M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})]\right)\big\}

\displaystyle\times\left\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})]\right\}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r_{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(4+4r_{n}\right)M^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+\left|\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|^{2}\right]=O\left(r_{n}\right).

For the last term in (
67
), there is

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Big\}^{2}-\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big\}^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}\Big|

\displaystyle\times\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}\Big|.

Compute that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}\left\{\frac{\eta}{2},\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}\left\{\frac{\eta}{2},r_{n}\big[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\big]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\big[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\big]^{2}\exp\frac{\eta r_{n}}{2}\big[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\big]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\eta^{-2}r_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\eta\big[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\big]\right\}=O(r_{n}^{2}),

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}\left\{\frac{\eta}{8},\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}\left\{\frac{\eta}{8},2|\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|+r_{n}\big[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\big]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 64\eta^{-2}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\frac{\eta}{4}\big[2|\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|+r_{n}M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+r_{n}{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\big]\right\}=O(1).

Applying Lemma
2
to each averaged term, we have

\Big|\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Big\}^{2}-\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big\}^{2}\Big|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{r_{n}p\log n}{n}\right).

(69)

Lastly we shall focus on the term
T
in (
67
). For each
i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}
, denote

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathfrak{X}}_{i}:=&\left\{X_{i}\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})<\eta^{-1}(\gamma+2)p\log n,\\
|\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|<\eta^{-1}(\gamma+2)\log n\end{array}\right\},\\
Y_{i}:=&\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\}^{2}-\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}^{2},\\
\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}:=&Y_{i}{\mathbb{I}}({\mathfrak{X}}_{i})-{\mathbb{E}}\{Y_{i}{\mathbb{I}}({\mathfrak{X}}_{i})\},\quad\widetilde{Y}_{i}:=Y_{i}{\mathbb{I}}({\mathfrak{X}}_{i}^{c})-{\mathbb{E}}\{Y_{i}{\mathbb{I}}({\mathfrak{X}}_{i}^{c})\}.\end{array}

We can compute that

\displaystyle\big|{\mathbb{E}}\{Y_{1}{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}^{c}_{1})\}\big|=

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\}^{2}{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}^{c}_{1})-\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}^{2}{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}^{c}_{1})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big\{2|\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|+\big(M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})]\big)\big\}

\displaystyle\times\left\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})]\right\}{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}^{c}_{1})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big|\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\big|^{2}{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}^{c}_{1})+4\big|M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\big|^{2}{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}^{c}_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(n^{-2})=o(r_{n}).

Then from Assumption
D
we know
