\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\widetilde{Y}_{i}\right|>\big|{\mathbb{E}}\{Y_{1}{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}^{c}_{1})\}\big|,\;{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\Big(\bigcup_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}{\mathfrak{X}}_{i}^{c}\Big)\bigcap\,{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}

(70)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\geq\eta^{-1}(\gamma+2)p\log n\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma p}).

Thus we have

\displaystyle\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}\right|=\left|Y_{1}{\mathbb{I}}({\mathfrak{X}}_{1})-\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+{\mathbb{E}}\{Y_{1}{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}^{c}_{1})\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big|\big\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\big\}\big\{\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\big\}\Big|{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}_{1})

\displaystyle+O\left(r_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r_{n}\left[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\right]\Big(2|\nabla f_{l}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|+r_{n}\big[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\big]\Big){\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}_{1})+O\left(r_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{2(\gamma+2)}{\eta}r_{n}\log n+\frac{(\gamma+2)}{\eta}r_{n}^{2}\,p\log n\right\}\left[M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})+{\mathbb{E}}\{M_{\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{l})\}\right]{\mathbb{I}}(\,{\mathfrak{X}}_{1})+O\left(r_{n}\right).

From Assumption
G
and
D
we know
r_{n}\log n+r_{n}^{2}\,p\log n=O(1)
, thus

{\mathfrak{E}}\left(\frac{\eta}{2},\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{1}\right)=O\left(r^{2}_{n}\log^{2}n+r_{n}^{4}\,p^{2}\log^{2}n\right)=O(1).

Then Lemma
2
yields

{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}\right|\geq C\left(r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log^{3}n}{n}}+r_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p^{3}\log^{3}n}{n}}\right)\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma p}),

(71)

holds for some
C>0
large enough. Thus from (
70
) and (
71
) we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}Y_{i}\right|\geq C\left(r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log^{3}n}{n}}+r_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p^{3}\log^{3}n}{n}}+r_{n}\right),\;{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}

(72)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$Y$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{i}\right|\geq C\left(r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log^{3}n}{n}}+r_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p^{3}\log^{3}n}{n}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\widetilde{Y}_{i}\right|>Cr_{n},\;{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}\right\}=O(n^{-\gamma p}).

Finally taking (
68
), (
69
) and (
72
) back to (
67
), we conclude that, under
{\mathfrak{X}}_{0}
, there is

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log^{3}n}{n}}+r_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p^{3}\log^{3}n}{n}}+r_{n}\right)=O\left(r_{n}\right),

since we already supposed
p=O(\sqrt{n}\log^{-1}n)
and
r_{n}^{2}\,p\log n=O(1)
in Assumption
G
.
∎

Lemma 12
.

(Stability of correction terms) Let
N
(
=(m+1)n
) i.i.d. random variables
X_{1},...,X_{N}
evenly distributed in
m+1
subsets
{\mathcal{H}}_{0},...,{\mathcal{H}}_{m}
. Let

\displaystyle\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{j}=

\displaystyle\,n^{-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}X_{i},

\displaystyle\widehat{X}=

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{med}(\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{j}\mid 0\leq j\leq m),

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{2}=

\displaystyle\,n^{-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}(X_{i}-\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{0})^{2}.

Suppose
X_{1}
satisfies
{\mathbb{E}}(X_{1})=0,\mathrm{Var}(X_{1})=\sigma^{2}
and
{\mathbb{E}}[e^{\eta|X_{1}|}]\leq C
. Then there exists
\widetilde{C}>0
sufficiently large, such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$X$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,_{j}-\widehat{X}-\frac{\widehat{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}\geq\widetilde{C}\max\left\{\frac{\log n}{m},\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\right\}\right]\leq O(n^{-\gamma}),

provided
\max\{1/(n\delta_{n}),\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\}=O(1)
.

Proof.

Construct the set

{\mathfrak{X}}_{Q}\triangleq\left\{X_{1},...,X_{N}\,\middle|\,\widehat{\sigma}\leq\sqrt{n},\,|\widehat{X}|\leq\sqrt{n}\right\}.

From Lemma
4
and Lemma
9
, it is easy to see
{\mathfrak{X}}_{Q}
holds with probability greater than
1-O(n^{-\gamma})
. Construct an
n^{-2}
-net
{\mathfrak{N}}_{Q}
for the square
\Theta_{Q}\triangleq[0,\sqrt{n}]\times[-\sqrt{n},\sqrt{n}]
, then there is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\widehat{X}-\frac{\widehat{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\sigma,y)\in\Theta_{Q}}\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-y-\frac{\sigma\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{(\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{y})\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{Q}}\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|Y_{j}-\widetilde{y}-\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle+\max_{(\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{y})\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{Q}}\sup_{\{(\sigma,y):|\widetilde{\sigma}-\sigma|,|\widetilde{y}-y|\leq n^{-2}\}}\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\left[{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\widetilde{y}-\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}-{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-y-\frac{\sigma\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{(\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{y})\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{Q}}\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\widetilde{y}-\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq 2\delta_{n}\right\}.

Denote

\displaystyle Z_{j}(y,\sigma)=:{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-y-\frac{\sigma\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq 2\delta_{n}\right\}-{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-y-\frac{\sigma\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq 2\delta_{n}\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{j}(y,\sigma)=:{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-y-\frac{\sigma\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq 2\delta_{n}\right\}.

Apply Lemma
1
to
\sqrt{n}Y_{j}
, we know there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{j}(y,\sigma)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\left|{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)-\sqrt{n}y-\sigma\Delta_{k}\right|\leq 2\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\right\}+\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq 4C_{1}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}\left\{1,Z_{j}(y,\sigma)\right\}\leq e\left\{1+{\mathbb{P}}_{j}(y,\sigma)\right\}{\mathbb{P}}_{j}(y,\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8ec\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\leq C_{2}\max\left\{\frac{\log n}{m},\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\right\}.

Apply Lemma
2
together with
|{\mathfrak{N}}_{Q}|\leq 2n^{5}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{(\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{y})\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{Q}}\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}Z_{j}(\widetilde{y},\widetilde{\sigma})\geq x\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n^{5}\sup_{(\sigma,y)\in\Theta_{Q}}{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}Z_{j}(y,\sigma)\geq x\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n^{-\gamma},

by taking
x\geq(\gamma+6)\sqrt{C_{2}}\max\left\{\frac{\log n}{m},\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\right\}
. Then we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$X$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{j}-\widehat{X}-\frac{\widehat{\sigma}\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{(\widetilde{\sigma},\widetilde{y})\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{Q}}\frac{2}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}Z_{j}(\widetilde{y},\widetilde{\sigma})+4c\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4(\gamma+6)\sqrt{C_{2}}\max\left\{\frac{\log n}{m},\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\right\},

holds with probability larger than
1-O(n^{-\gamma})
. Thus we can obtain the desired result.
∎

C.3
Proofs of results in Appendix
C.1.2

Now, we are ready to provide the proofs of the convergence rate and asymptotic normality for the RCSL estimator.

Proof of Theorem
5
and Theorem
6
.

Denote
b_{n}
as the desired convergence rate of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}
, and
\boldsymbol{g}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=n^{-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}\nabla f(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})
. We construct the following sets in the parameter space.

\displaystyle\Theta_{0}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}\leq r_{n}\},

(73)

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}=b_{n}\},

\displaystyle\Theta_{2}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}\leq b_{n}\}.

Given an initial estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}
lies in the set
\Theta_{0}
, we will show

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}f(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}),\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\rangle>\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}f(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}),\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\rangle,

(74)

holds uniformly for
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\in\Theta_{1}
with probability tending to 1. Notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\{f(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-f(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}-\left\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}),\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle

(75)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\int_{0}^{1}\big[\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\big]{\mathrm{d}}s,\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\int_{0}^{1}\left\{\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}{\mathrm{d}}s,\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})+\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}),\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)\left\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))-\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}{\mathrm{d}}s}_{\boldsymbol{T}_{1}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1}\Big[\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big]{\mathrm{d}}s}_{\boldsymbol{T}_{2}},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\underbrace{\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}_{\boldsymbol{T}_{3}},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle+\left\langle\underbrace{\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})}_{\boldsymbol{T}_{4}},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\right\rangle.

To show positivity of this difference, it left to bound the norms of
\boldsymbol{T}_{1},\boldsymbol{T}_{2},\boldsymbol{T}_{3}
and
\boldsymbol{T}_{4}
for
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}\in\Theta_{0}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\in\Theta_{1}
uniformly.

Firstly, from assumption
C
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{T}_{1}\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)\left\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))-\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\|dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}C_{H}(1-s)s|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}{\mathrm{d}}s=\frac{C_{H}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}}{6}=o(1).

To control the rest three terms, we denote

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Z}(x,\boldsymbol{\theta}):=

\displaystyle\nabla f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta})-\nabla f(x,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),

(76)

for ease of notations. Then we will show

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}}\right).

(77)

Construct the
(r_{n}n^{-M})
-net
{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}
for the set
\Theta_{0}
, where
M>0
is some sufficiently large number. From Lemma 5.2 of
Vershynin 2010
we know
{\mathrm{C}ard}({\mathfrak{N}}_{0})\leq(1+2n^{M})^{p}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}r_{n}^{-1}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}r_{n}^{-1}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right|_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{M+1}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{i},\boldsymbol{v})\right\}+\frac{1}{n^{M}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})\right\}.

From (
48b
) in Assumption
D
and Markov inequality, there is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{M}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})\right\}\leq\frac{p^{\gamma_{0}}}{n^{M}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\exp\left\{p^{-\gamma_{0}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})\right\}\right]<\frac{C_{M}p^{\gamma_{0}}}{n^{M}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{M+1}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\{\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(X_{i},\boldsymbol{v})\right\}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{p^{\gamma_{0}}\log n}{n^{M}}\right).

On the other hand, by standard
\epsilon
-net argument for vector norms, we know that there exists a
1/2
-net
{\mathfrak{N}}_{S}
of
{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}
such that
{\mathrm{C}ard}({\mathfrak{N}}_{S})\leq 5^{p}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{S}}\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle+\sup_{|\boldsymbol{v}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}|_{2}\leq 1/2}\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}),\boldsymbol{v}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{S}}\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle.

Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}r_{n}^{-1}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right|_{2}\geq 2x\right)\leq

\displaystyle(1+2n^{M})^{p}\max_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}r_{n}^{-1}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right|_{2}\geq 2x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 5^{p}(1+2n^{M})^{p}\max_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\langle r_{n}^{-1}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\geq x\right).

Moreover, by (
48a
) in Assumption
D
, for every
\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}
, we can compute that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}\left\{\eta/2,\left\langle r_{n}^{-1}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{1},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{M_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})+{\mathbb{E}}[M_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})]\right\}^{2}\exp\frac{\eta}{2}\left\{M_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})+{\mathbb{E}}[M_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})]\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\eta^{-2}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp\eta\big\{M_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})+{\mathbb{E}}[M_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}}(X_{1},\boldsymbol{v})]\big\}\big]\leq 4C_{M}^{2}\eta^{-2}=O\left(1\right).

So Lemma
2
yields

\displaystyle 5^{p}(1+2n^{M})^{p}\max_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in{\mathfrak{N}}_{0}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\left\langle r_{n}^{-1}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}),\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle\geq x\right)=O(n^{-p\gamma}),

with
x=C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}}
and
C_{1}
large enough. Hence we proved the bound (
77
). Similarly we can give bounds for
\boldsymbol{T}_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{T}_{3}
as follows

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{T}_{2}|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))\right|_{2}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{2}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|_{2}=O\left(b_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}}\right);

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{T}_{3}|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|_{2}=O\left(r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}}\right).

Now for the term
\boldsymbol{T}_{4}
, under the rates constraints in Assumption
G
, we can prove a similar result as (
77
):

\displaystyle\big|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\big|_{2}

(78)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{\sqrt{p}\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right).

However, the proof of (
78
) involves more delicate analysis, so we delegate this part to Lemma
13
below. Moreover, follow the proof of Theorem
1
together with Assumption
E
and
F
, we can apply exponential inequality (Lemma
2
) to the i.i.d. terms in (
36
) and yields

\displaystyle|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{p^{1/2}\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}\right).

Note that here we have a
\sqrt{\log n}
in the second term because of the diverging dimension
p
. Thus we have

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{T}_{4}|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|_{2}+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\big|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{p^{1/2}\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right),

Thus there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}
such that

\left\|\boldsymbol{T}_{1}\right\|=o(1),\quad\text{and}\quad|\boldsymbol{T}_{2}|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{T}_{3}|_{2}+|\boldsymbol{T}_{4}|_{2}\leq\widetilde{C}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{p^{1/2}\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right).

Now in the view of Assumption
B
, we continue with (
75
), there is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}}\{f(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-f(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}-\langle\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}),\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\boldsymbol{T}_{1}\right\|\cdot|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}^{2}-|\boldsymbol{T}_{2}|_{2}\cdot|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}-|\boldsymbol{T}_{3}|_{2}\cdot|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}-|\boldsymbol{T}_{4}|_{2}\cdot|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{0}b_{n}^{2}}{2}-o\left(b_{n}^{2}\right)-\widetilde{C}b_{n}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{p^{1/2}\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right)>0,

provided
b_{n}=O(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{p^{1/2}\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}})
. So we have (
74
) holds true, and thus

|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{p^{1/2}\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right),

which proves Theorem
6
. Apply this formula inductively, we can obtain Theorem
5
.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Let
\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta})
be defined as (
18
), with
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}
replaced by
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. Then under the Assumption
A
-
G
, there is

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\big|\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.10216pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.02228pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.02228pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\big|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+\frac{\sqrt{p}\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right).

Proof.

First of all we need to split it into three parts:

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}}_{T_{41}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\frac{3\sqrt{p}}{m\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Big|\left\{\widehat{\sigma_{l}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Big[{\mathbb{I}}\Big\{g_{j,l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big\}-\frac{k}{K+1}\Big]\Big|}_{T_{42}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\frac{3\sqrt{p}}{m\sqrt{n}}\Big|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Big[{\mathbb{I}}\Big\{g_{j,l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big\}-{\mathbb{I}}\Big\{g_{j,l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\leq\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big\}\Big]\Big|}_{T_{43}},

where the factor
3
comes from the fact
1/\psi(0)=\sqrt{2\pi}<3
. For the first term
T_{41}
, rehash the proof of equation (
77
) we can easily obtain

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}Z_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|\geq Cr_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}}\right\}

(79)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}\langle\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta}),\boldsymbol{e}_{l}\rangle\right|\geq Cr_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}}\right\}\leq mpn^{-\gamma p}.

where
Z_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})
is the
l
’th coordinate of
\boldsymbol{Z}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})
defined in (
76
). Then from Lemma
8
(with
q=1/2
and
\alpha=\alpha_{n}
), we know

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}Z_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\left|\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},(1-2\alpha_{n})/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},1/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}Z_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|+2\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max\Big\{\Big|\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},(1-2\alpha_{n})/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big|,

\displaystyle\Big|\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},1/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}\Big|\Big\},

where
\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},(1-2\alpha_{n})/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},1/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
represent the
(1-2\alpha_{n})/(2-2\alpha_{n})
-th and
1/(2-2\alpha_{n})
-th quantile of non-Byzantine machines respectively. For the additional term, we can follow the proof of Lemma
4
and show

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max\Big\{\Big|\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},(1-2\alpha_{n})/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big|,\,\Big|\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},1/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big|\Big\}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{mn}}\right),

provided condition
\alpha_{n}\leq 1/2-\delta
holds for some
\delta\in(0,1/2)
. Therefore we have

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{mn}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right).

(80)

Taking all coordinate together we have

\left|T_{41}\right|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right).

(81)

The rate of
T_{42}
hinges on the uniform rate of
\widehat{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})
over
\Theta_{0}
. Indeed, in Lemma
11
we proved

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(r_{n}\right),

(82)

provided
p=O(\sqrt{n}\log^{-1}n)
in Assumption
G
. So this yields

\left|T_{42}\right|\leq\frac{3\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{r_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(83)

It left to deal with the term
T_{43}
. From (
79
), (
80
), (
82
) and (
62
), we know there exists a constant
C
large enough, such that the following inequalities holds uniformly:

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}

\displaystyle\left|g_{l,j}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-g_{l,j}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\leq Cr_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}},

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\leq C\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{mn}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}}\right),

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{0}}

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\Delta_{k}n^{-1/2}\leq C\frac{r_{n}}{\sqrt{n}},

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|\leq C,

with probability higher than
1-O(n^{-\gamma})
. Under this event, using Lemma
12
with

\delta_{n}=C\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{mn}}+3r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{n}}\right),

we have

\displaystyle\left|T_{43}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j=0}^{m}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|g_{j,l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{C\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\left|g_{j,l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Delta_{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}+r_{n}\sqrt{\frac{p^{2}\log n}{n}}\right),

(84)

holds with probability larger than
1-O(pn^{-\gamma})
. Combining (
81
), (
83
) and (
84
), the lemma is proved.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

When the iteration number satisfies (
24
), and the rate constraints satisfies
\alpha_{n}=o(1\sqrt{mp})
and
p=o(\min\{\frac{n^{1/3}}{\log^{2/3}n},\frac{m^{1/2}}{\log^{3/2}n}\})
, we clearly know that

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)}\in\Theta_{t}:=\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{p\log n}{mn}}\right\},

with high probability, for some
C
sufficiently large. Moreover, we need sharper rate for
|\boldsymbol{T}_{4}|_{2}
in (
75
). Indeed, from Lemma
8
we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{t}}\left|\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\widehat{g}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\max_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta_{t}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{H}}_{j}}Z_{l}(X_{i},\boldsymbol{\theta})\right|+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\left|\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},(1-2\alpha_{n})/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},1/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|.

Note that
1/(2-2\alpha_{n})-(1-2\alpha_{n})/(2-2\alpha_{n})=o(1/\sqrt{mp})
, by Lemma
7
we have sharper constraint on the quantile gap

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\left|\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},(1-2\alpha_{n})/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\widehat{g}_{l}^{{\mathcal{B}},1/(2-2\alpha_{n})}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}\right).

Therefore we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\widehat{\boldsymbol{g}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n}+\frac{\sqrt{p}\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+\frac{p^{3/2}\log n}{\sqrt{m}n}\right).

Then follow the proof of Lemma
13
we have

\displaystyle|\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|_{2}

(85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n}+\frac{\sqrt{p}\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+\frac{p^{3/2}\log n}{\sqrt{m}n}\right)=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}}\right).

Now we start to prove asymptotic normality. From equations (
1
) and (
20
), we know

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=0,\quad\text{and }\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)})=\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)}).

Therefore, from (
85
) and (
77
) there is

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)})-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})

\displaystyle+\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t)})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}}\right).

(86)

On the other hand, from Assumption
C
and equation (
77
)

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)})-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}

(87)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)})-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\cdot(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})+\int_{0}^{1}\left\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+s(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}))-\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}{\mathrm{d}}s\cdot(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})

\displaystyle+\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)})-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\mu}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\cdot(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{p\log n}{\sqrt{m}n}\right)=\left|\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\cdot(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}}\right).

We can combine (
86
) and (
87
), rearrange the terms, then there is

\left|\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\cdot(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}=\left|\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$\boldsymbol{g}$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right|_{2}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}}\right).

(88)

Denote

\displaystyle G_{j,l}(x)={\mathbb{P}}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}g_{j,l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}{\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}\leq x\right\},

\displaystyle\quad I_{j,l}(x)={\mathbb{I}}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}g_{j,l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}{\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}\leq x\right\}.

(89)

Then from (
36
), for every entry we have

\displaystyle\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$g$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})

(90)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}{\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\{I_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})-G_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})\}+O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{\log n}{m\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\log^{3/4}n}{n^{1/2}m^{3/4}}\right).

From equations (
88
), (
90
) and the rates constrains, for any vector
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
, there is

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\cdot(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{j\notin{\mathcal{B}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{p}\left\{\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})I_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})-\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})G_{j,l}(\Delta_{k})\right\}\widetilde{v}_{l}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mn}}\right).

Now we apply central limit theorem and yield

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{(m+1)n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}}}\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\cdot(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\rangle\xrightarrow{d}{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

\displaystyle\text{where }\quad\widetilde{\sigma}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}}^{2}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\rm T}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}.

Here
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times p}
has its
(l_{1},l_{2})
-entry defined as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{l_{1},l_{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{l_{1}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\sigma_{l_{2}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}{\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})\}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\{I_{0,l_{1}}(\Delta_{k})-G_{0,l_{1}}(\Delta_{k})\}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\{I_{0,l_{2}}(\Delta_{k})-G_{0,l_{2}}(\Delta_{k})\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}}{\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\psi(\Delta_{k})\}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\left\{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}g_{0,{l_{1}}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{1},l_{1}}}}\leq\Delta_{k_{1}},\frac{\sqrt{n}g_{0,{l_{2}}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{l_{2},l_{2}}}}\leq\Delta_{k_{2}}\right)-G_{0,l_{1}}(\Delta_{k_{1}})G_{0,l_{2}}(\Delta_{k_{2}})\right\},

since
\sigma_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\{\nabla f_{l}(X,\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}}=\sqrt{\sigma_{l,l}}
. Moreover, we can apply multivariate Berry-Esseen theorem (See Theorem 1.3 in
Götze 1991
) and give

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{l_{1},l_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{l_{1},l_{2}}+O(n^{-1/2}),

where
\mathcal{C}_{l_{1},l_{2}}
is defined in (
14
). Now we replace
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}
with
\left\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\right\}^{-1}\boldsymbol{v}
. From Assumption
B
we know the norm of
\boldsymbol{v}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
is rescaled by a factor of constant order. Thus the theorem is proved.
∎

Appendix D
Examples Verification

In this appendix, we will verify that a large class of generalized linear models and M-estimators satisfy our proposed Assumptions
A
–
F
.

D.1
Generalized linear models

For a generalized linear models (GLM) with the canonical link function
{\mathcal{L}}:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
, each
i.i.d.
observation
(\boldsymbol{X},Y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p+1}
admits the following conditional probability function

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Y\mid\boldsymbol{X})=\widetilde{c}\exp\left\{\frac{Y\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle-{\mathcal{L}}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)}{c(\sigma)}\right\},

(91)

where
\widetilde{c}
and
c(\sigma)
are some constants,
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
is the true parameter. The loss function based on the maximum likelihood estimator is defined by,

f(Y,\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta})=-Y\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+{\mathcal{L}}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle).

(92)

Then we have the following proposition.

Proposition 2
.

Let
(\boldsymbol{X},Y)
be observation of a generalized linear model (
91
) with a convex link function
{\mathcal{L}}
. Suppose the following condition holds:

(C1)

There exists
\rho_{0}>0
such that

\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}\geq\rho_{0};

(C2)

There exists
M>0
such that

|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq M,\quad|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{1})-{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{2})|\leq M|x_{1}-x_{2}|;

(C3)

There exists
\eta,C_{M}>0
such that

\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left\{\eta|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}\right]\leq C_{M},\quad{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\eta|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)-Y|^{2}\right]\leq C_{M}.

Then the loss function defined in (
92
) satisfies Assumption
A
-
F
.

Condition (C1) and (C3) imply that the covariate
\boldsymbol{X}
is a non-degenerate subgaussian vector. And another part of condition (C3) shows that
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)-Y={\mathbb{E}}[Y|\boldsymbol{X}]-Y
has a subgaussian tail. For convenience of verification, we assume the
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}
to be Lipschitz continuous in Condition (C2).

Proof of Proposition
2
.

Firstly we can compute the gradient and Hessian as follows,

\displaystyle\nabla f(Y,\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta})=-Y\boldsymbol{X}+\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)\boldsymbol{X},\quad

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})={\mathbb{E}}\big\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)\boldsymbol{X}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)\boldsymbol{X}\big\},

\displaystyle\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})={\mathbb{E}}\left\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)\boldsymbol{XX}^{{\mathrm{T}}}\right\}.\quad

Then we can verify these assumptions one by one.

•

Assumption
B
: Compute that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\max}\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}=

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\leq\frac{M}{\eta}C_{M},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\min}\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}=

\displaystyle\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\rho_{0}.

•

Assumption
C
: By elementary inequalities
3|xy^{2}|\leq|x|^{3}+2|y|^{3}
and
2|x|^{3}\leq e^{x^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\big|\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)-\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)\big|\cdot|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{|\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|\cdot|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left(|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{3}\right)|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}|_{2}\leq\frac{MC_{M}}{2\eta^{3/2}}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}|_{2}.

•

Assumption
D
:

\displaystyle M_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}}(Y,\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{v})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}|_{2}}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle\left\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|\cdot\left|\left\langle\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}}{|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}|_{2}},\,\boldsymbol{X}\right\rangle\right|.

Thus

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left\{\frac{\eta}{M}M_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}}(Y,\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{v})\right\}\right]\leq\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\eta|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right)\right\}\leq C_{M}.

Similarly

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(Y,\boldsymbol{X},,\boldsymbol{v})\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}M|\boldsymbol{X}|_{2}\cdot\left|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle\right|\leq M|\boldsymbol{X}|_{2}^{2}=M\sum_{l=1}^{p}|\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{e}_{l}\rangle|^{2},

where
\boldsymbol{e}_{l}
is the
l
-th base vector. Then using generalized Hölder’s inequality we can prove

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}\exp\left\{\frac{\eta}{Mp}\,\hbox{\kern 0.09995pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.42082pt\hbox{\kern-1.00006pt$M$\kern-1.00006pt}}}\,(Y,\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{v})\right\}\right]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left\{\frac{\eta}{p}\sum_{l=1}^{p}|\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{e}_{l}\rangle|^{2}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{l=1}^{p}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\eta|\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{e}_{l}\rangle|^{2}\right)\right\}\right]^{1/p}\leq C_{M}.

•

Assumption
E
: The variance at
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}
is

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{-Y+\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)\right\}^{2}X_{l}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[c(\sigma)\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)X_{l}^{2}\right],

then we can bound them as follows

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\leq

\displaystyle c(\sigma)\Lambda_{\max}\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}\leq\eta^{-1}Mc(\sigma)C_{M},

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\geq

\displaystyle c(\sigma)\Lambda_{\min}\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}\geq\rho_{0}c(\sigma).

•

Assumption
F
: Using Cauchy inequality we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\exp\eta|\nabla f_{l}(Y,\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\exp\eta\left|\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)X_{l}-YX_{l}\right|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\eta|\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle)-Y|^{2}\right]{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\eta|\langle\boldsymbol{X}_{l},\boldsymbol{e}_{l}\rangle|^{2}\right]}\leq C_{M}.

∎

As an example, we can show that the logistic regression model satisfies these conditions.

Example 3
.

(Logistic regression) In logistic regression model, the response variable
Y
takes value in
\{0,1\}
, and the link function is
{\mathcal{L}}(x)=\log(1+e^{x})
. Then we can compute that

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{1+e^{-x}},\quad{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{(1+e^{x})(1+e^{-x})},\quad|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)|\leq 2.

It is not hard to verify that conditions (C1)–(C3) in Proposition
2
hold, provided non-degenerate subgaussian covariate
\boldsymbol{X}
.

D.2
M
-Estimator

Next we consider the M-estimator. Assume that each
i.i.d.
observation
(\boldsymbol{X},Y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p+1}
is generated from the linear model,

Y=\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle+\epsilon,

(93)

and the loss function is

f(Y,\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta})={\mathcal{L}}(Y-\langle\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle).

(94)

Then we have the following proposition.

Proposition 3
.

Let
(\boldsymbol{X},Y)
be observation of linear model (
93
) and
{\mathcal{L}}
is a convex regression function. Suppose the noise
\epsilon
is independent of the covariate
\boldsymbol{X}
, and
{\mathbb{E}}\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)\}=0
. Moreover the following conditions hold true:

(C1’)

There exists
\rho_{0}>0
such that

\min\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\epsilon)\},{\mathbb{E}}\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)^{2}\},\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}\geq\rho_{0};

(C2’)

There exists a constant
M>0
such that

|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq M,\qquad|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{1})-{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{2})|\leq M|x_{1}-x_{2}|;

(C3’)

There exists
\eta,C_{M}>0
such that

\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left\{\eta|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}\right]\leq C_{M},\quad{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left\{\eta|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)|^{2}\right\}\right]\leq C_{M}.

Then the loss function defined in (
94
) satisfies Assumptions
A
-
F
.

Again condition (C1’) implies the non-degeneracy of covariate
\boldsymbol{X}
. The first half of condition (C3’) requires covariate
\boldsymbol{X}
to be sub-gaussian. While the noise, depending on the explicit formulation of
{\mathcal{L}}
, can be heavy-tailed. As will be seen in the following, the noise has to be sub-gaussian in linear regression, but is allowed to be heavy-tailed in Huber regression. It is worthwhile noting that for the ease of presentation, in condition (C2’) we simply assume
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}
to be Lipschitz continuous. However, in Example
2
, we will prove that the Huber regression model satisfies all assumptions in Section
C.1.1
.

Proof of Proposition
3
.

We can directly compute the gradient and Hessian as follows:

\displaystyle\nabla f(Y,\boldsymbol{X},,\boldsymbol{\theta})={\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\rangle+\epsilon)\boldsymbol{X},

\displaystyle\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})={\mathbb{E}}\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\rangle+\epsilon)\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{X}^{\rm T}\}.

Now we verify those assumptions.

•

Assumption
B
:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\max}\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}=

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\epsilon)|\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm T}\boldsymbol{X}|^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\epsilon)\right\}\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}|\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm T}\boldsymbol{X}|^{2}\leq\eta^{-1}MC_{M},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\min}\{\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\}=

\displaystyle\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\epsilon)|\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm T}\boldsymbol{X}|^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\epsilon)\right\}\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}|\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm T}\boldsymbol{X}|^{2}\geq\rho^{2}_{0}.

•

Verification for Assumption
C
and
D
are almost the same as the proof in Proposition
2
, thus omitted for brevity.

•

Assumption
E
: The variance of the
l
’th coordinate is

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)^{2}X_{l}^{2}\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)^{2}\right]{\mathbb{E}}(X_{l}^{2}),

thus

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)^{2}\right]\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\{{\mathbb{E}}(X_{l}^{2})\}\leq\eta^{-2}C_{M}^{2},

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})\geq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)^{2}\right]\min_{1\leq l\leq p}\{{\mathbb{E}}(X_{l}^{2})\}\geq\rho_{0}^{2}.

•

Assumption
F
: Using Cauchy inequality we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp\eta|\nabla f_{l}(Y,\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})-\mu_{l}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*})|\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\exp\eta\left|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)X_{l}\right|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\eta|{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\epsilon)|^{2}\right]{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\eta|\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{e}_{l}\rangle|^{2}\right]}\leq C_{M}.

∎

Example
1
Continued.
In linear regression model, the regression function
{\mathcal{L}}
is defined by
{\mathcal{L}}(x)=x^{2}/2
. Then we can compute that
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(x)=x,{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x)=1
. It is relatively straightforward to verify that the conditions in Proposition
3
hold, provided
\boldsymbol{X}
is a non-degenerate sub-gaussian random vector and the noise
\epsilon
follows a zero-mean sub-gaussian distribution.

Example
2
Continued.
In Huber regression model, the regression function
{\mathcal{L}}
is defined by

{\mathcal{L}}(x)=\begin{cases}x^{2}/2\quad&\text{for }|x|\leq\delta,\\
\delta(|x|-\delta/2)\quad&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Then we can compute that

{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(x)=\begin{cases}x\quad&\text{for }|x|\leq\delta,\\
\delta\,\mathrm{sign}(x)\quad&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}\qquad{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x)={\mathbb{I}}(|x|\leq\delta).

In this case, Proposition
3
is not directly applicable since
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x)
is not Lipschitz continuous. However, if noise
\epsilon
has a symmetric distribution and uniformly bounded probability density function, Assumption
C
can be verified as follows.

Verification for Huber Regression.

We only need to show the Lipschitz Hessian assumption
C
holds true. It is easy to compute the Hessian matrix of Huber loss as follows:

\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})={\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbb{I}}(|\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\rangle+\epsilon|\leq\delta)\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{X}^{\rm T}\right\}.

Assume the noise
\epsilon
has probability density function
\mathfrak{p}(x)
uniformly bounded by a constant
M>0
, then by independence of
\epsilon
and
\boldsymbol{X}
, there is

\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta})={\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(|\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\rangle+\epsilon|\leq\delta\,\Big|\,\boldsymbol{X}\Big)\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{X}^{\rm T}\right\}.

Then for arbitrary
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-\nabla\boldsymbol{\mu}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\left\{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(|\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\rangle+\epsilon|\leq\delta\,\Big|\,\boldsymbol{X}\Big)-{\mathbb{P}}\Big(|\langle\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\rangle+\epsilon|\leq\delta\,\Big|\,\boldsymbol{X}\Big)\right\}|\boldsymbol{v}^{\rm T}\boldsymbol{X}|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2M\sup_{\boldsymbol{v}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{p-1}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{|\langle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|\cdot|\langle\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{X}\rangle|^{2}\right\}\leq\eta^{-3/2}MC_{M}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}|_{2}.

∎
